PREFACE 

In preparing this (Special Edition) upon the subject of Hypnotism, Mesmerism, Personal Magnetism, and Clairvoyance, the opportunity has been taken to make extensive revision, the progress made in the knowledge of OCCULT Science by the inhabitants of this country having enabled the writer to enlarge greatly upon the subject in general and to give less space to the combating of the various superstitions connected with the occult. 

Superstition is coeval in age with the world. At every period man has demanded from a supernatural power, authority, fortune, honors. At every period he has in vain attempted to decipher the incomprehensible books of fate and future. Vainly during centuries has he endeavored to upraise a corner of the dark veil which so well conceals the mysteries of the OCCULT. 

Very few have ever succeeded in lifting a fragment of this veil during the long course of ages. The history of 

Hypnotism, Mesmerism and Magnetism is a sad recital, though oftentimes ridiculous yet ever interesting because of the desperate efforts of this materialistic race to form an affinity or claim an affiliation or relationship between Hypnotism, Mesmerism, and the veritable. 

Black Art, White and Black magic, Witchcraft, Sor cery, Necromancy, or the raising of the dead; Spiritualism, Diabolical Magic, or the evocation of evil spirits by virtue of a compact made with them, etc., etc.

HYPNOTISM AND MAGNETISM. 

As to the reality of the above or the conjuration of the evil spirits of the Astral Plane, which means practically a full realization of "Faust and the Demon," the writer will say that there are many weird practices, almost too strange to believe. Yet because one is ignorant of their existence, it does not follow that they are not real, as the sad records of sorcery and witchcraft, of Voodooism and Black Magic abundantly testify. 

Man does not know himself, therefore he does not understand those things of the inner spiritual, or occult world. However, it is not my intention to enlarge upon 

OCCULT Philosophy here, only to state that Hypnotism, Mesmerism, Personal Magnetism, Fascination, etc., have no relation whatever to Black Art; and to disabuse the reader's mind of this idea which is so prevalent among the unlearned, that Hypnotism bears any kin whatever to the doings of the witch of Endor, with whom Saul, before going to combat the Philistines, held consultation, and who raised at his solicitation, the shade or astral body of Samuel. Neither has it anything to do with the soothsayers, Buddhism, Brahmanism, or the Astrologers who held forth among the Chaldeans, Tribes of Israel, etc. It is the object of the writer to annihilate this erroneous idea existing among the students of Practical Psychology, and in the near future to give to the public a work on OCCULT Philosophy setting forth the Oriental tradition, the secrets taught in India, China and throughout the Orient, embracing a full explanation of the above, including Alchemy, Occult Philosophy and Natural Philosophy.

The lessons and instructions contained herein are

PREFACE 

entirely new and the method of investigation and application suggested is the one which appears to be most likely to yield results of permanent value. The author, having spent a number of years in the Orient among the Hindoo adepts of India, has devoted many years of his life to scientific research, the unfolding and understanding of the unseen and invisible Occult Forces. A large portion of which time has been passed as a demonstrator, lecturer and general Practitioner of Psycho Therapeutics, having but one general object in view; that is, to be of the greatest benefit, and give the best service possible to the people and the world in which he lives. 

By extensive research he is satisfied that this science dates far back in the history of the world. The tribes of Israel, the Chaldeans, the grand Zoroastrian nation, the Hindoo-worship which believes Krishna to have been God incarnate, the dismal funeral Pile, the worthy Prelate and Priests of antiquity who thought it preferable to use concentration (Hypnotic force) in leading back sheep wandered from the fold, that sacred volume, the Bible, all reveal in numerous incidents and magical practices, which leaves no doubt in the mind of the student of Occult Lore that Hypnotic, that mysterious unseen force, is the Basic Principle and foundation of all Occult Science. Back in the ages when religion and science walked hand in hand in a most Divine harmony as counterparts in one grand whole, it is found not only in Hebraic and Christian Bible, but in all the sacred temples and monasteries of India, that there is a wonderful and magic virtue in the mind or mental force, and that this invisible element can be projected, as it were, from the body when the forces of the soul are wrought

by focussing the powers of concentration. Whence there are made most patent procreations, and most famous impressions, and strong effects.

The importance of a thorough knowledge of this science will most certainly be appreciated by the wise student; and it is worthy of years of critical study and experiment. It enables the Parent to control and guide the child, and is a guide to all persons in the preservation of health and strength, the key to and only aid in success, and to the attainment of the greatest desires and objects in life. It is the only means of prolonging the life of old and young. To physicians it is essential, and of the utmost importance. Prominent Practitioners, who have been taught this system have, we know, wisely prophesied that the time is near when a physician will not be tolerated or considered qualified to receive a diploma without a thorough knowledge of Psycho- 

Therapeutics. 

The science of Hypnotism, and Psycho-Therapeutics (which is now emerging from the limbo of quackery, because for years the Medical Profession allowed it to be exploited by "Professors" who used it as a universal remedy in all cases, suitable or unsuitable), is attracting the respectful attention of the profession and the thoughtful public. The many authentic and well attested accounts of its phenomena, which at times have been so extraordinary and seemingly bordered so closely on the miraculous, have aroused intense interest in them. 

And as these statements and phenomena have been attested to by men of science, citizens and newspaper reporters of reputation, and as it has been found that Hypnotism and suggestion, both mental and verbal,

PREFACE 

have such enormous Therapeutic possibilities before. them, it is essential that serious attention should be given to the study of medical psychology and Hypnosis, by those interested in scientific medicine and mental science.

This research was, however, formerly commenced in this country with feelings of great distrust and reluctance, as it was notorious that for years unprincipled persons and Charlatans had imposed on the public by pretending to all manner of Occult Powers as Mesmerists. These pretenders had so offended by this means the feelings of men of science and good minded people and had caused such a disgust of these Phenomena that many felt it was impossible to investigate Hypnotism without contamination. In addition to this, the subject was discredited because there were two schools in the field with opposing theories, observations, and practice, whose pupils carried on a bitter warfare in the press. 

The most contradictory statements on matters of observation had been made by distinguished Physicians, and Phenomena had also been recorded which, if true, seemed to overturn the established laws of nature and all our fixed ideas of things. But in spite of the antipathy produced by these facts, one instinctively felt that underlying this Mass and Maze of contradiction, absurdity and imposture there lay important truths; and so, impelled by a mixture of scientific and idle curiosity many have turned investigators to find from personal experience and observation what Hypnotism was, and to what extent it could be used to benefit humanity if applied in medicine and education.

While feeling that the intelligent student of Hypnotism will greatly appreciate a systematic treatise upon

the subject by one who differs widely from the theories and teachings of the accepted schools of materialistic physiological psychology, the author cannot but expect that some conclusions contained herein will not be received without criticism; nevertheless they have not been advanced without consideration; and whilst, doubtless, there may be errors in the manner of representation, the essential principles here laid down for the student and practitioner are sound and correct ones. 

They are, as far as possible, the result of personal experience and observation, methods of introspection and purely theoretical speculation having been eliminated.

It has been a subject of ancient dispute whether or not the human mind is endowed with the faculty of exerting magnetic influence or OCCULT force over the mentality of another, and if this Magnetic virtue can be accumulated, concentrated, projected, and transported so as to take action at a remote distance, without the aid of any intermediate substance.

The observing student of Psychology will notice that the theories of Vital Magnetism gain force and precision in each successive writer. That the theory is the correct

one all ancient philosophies of note agree. When such men as Paracelsus, Henry Corneils Agrippa, Albertus 

Magnus, Raymond Lully, Doctor Dee and Sir Edward

Kelly, advocate and prove their conclusions of the doctrine of animal or personal or vital magnetism, it is certainly amusing to listen to some of the silly and nonsensical theories set up by the modern Physiologists, anatomists, pathologists and a host of other material and agnostic superficial thinkers, who hold that body, or the combination of matter, produces thought, intelligence, consciousness, mind or soul. Their denial of Hypnosis, thought transference and Clairvoyance is never taken seriously by the wise student, therefore this work has no space to devote to the consideration of those who attribute the hypnotic sleep to exhaustion of the optic nerves, or are led to the conclusion by these materialistic ideas, that the induction of hypnosis is due to physiological modification of the nervous system. 

Their assumption that the state produced is a supernormal physiological one, induced by a physical or appreciable action on the nervous system, instantly relegates them to that crude class of materialistic thinkers who are still wandering in the gloomy mists of materialism. All the world knows from ancient times that there have been atheistic and agnostic thinkers. Even in 

India we have the materialistic Charvaka who denies the existence of the soul after death or that it has action independent of the physical body. They believe that the body is the soul, and that the soul does not maintain an existence outside of the body, and that when the body dies, the soul is also dead and gone. According to this class of thinkers, those who believe in

13 Personal Magnetism or mental force or the existence of the soul as separate from the body, or in life after death are ignorant and superstitious fools, while those that tack on their materialistic ideas, (the fruits of superficial observation of the subject), are clever and intelligent beings. Most of them hold that there is no such thing as a soul. No argument or evidence can convince them or change their views, because they will not admit the existence of anything which does not serve to defend and sustain their materialistic Propositions. Such narrow-minded so-called scientists are of little or no assistance in the advancement of mental or spiritual science, their narrow views totally unfitting them to study scientifically the human mind or mental forces, their possibilities and Physical expression. Thus they can never become familiar with the more abstruse subjects in natural Phenomena. 

It certainly seems unlikely that a definite knowledge of the nature of the mind of man, its conditions and possibilities can be for long entirely unknown to all. 

Yet Sectarian bigots, ignorant enthusiasts and religious hypocrites say it is wrong to desire to know anything of 

Hypnotism, Spiritualism and other OCCULT subjects. 

Is not knowledge of spiritual or soul science essential?

Has not the Omniscient Creator implanted these as well as other desires? And has He not contrived and supplied a knowledge of this science to all sincere, unprejudiced seekers after truth? If knowledge is power and of paramount importance to man's welfare the wise man will not be influenced or turned aside from a perusal or study of OCCULT Phenomena by modern material istic scientists and religious cants, who are willing to

follow blind and deceptive chance, which they blasphemingly call "Providence."

These are the people who have strained at a gnat and swallowed a camel. When these people in their materialistic bark are wrecked upon the turbulent stream of life, they will realize that Providence, and common sense (sense that is not common) should have been their compass. It is then that they will reflect honor on the cause and science of Practical Psychology, instead of stigma and reproach.

This subtle, silent, intangible and invisible psychic or soul force (Hypnotic Influence), is the power that has been conserved to the use of the strong minded; and when concentrated, and projected by the will with a combative, subduing, or controlling intent, it becomes the magic wand that rules the social and commercial world, for it triumphs over obstacles and brings success in business, social, and married life.

It is that marvelous, subtle, unseen, silent influence, element or power that enables one to control man, woman, child or beast at will. A person of ordinary intelligence, and strong mind having this Psychological or OCCULT power well developed, is able to control those having no knowledge of Mental Science, so that he actually projects into their minds his own desires, feelings, thoughts and habits to the suspension of their power to control themselves by their own free-wills. 

The present stage of social and commercial life is one of combat-man pitted against man. The invisible weapon used in this mental battle or struggle for supremacy is psychic power, mental or OCCULT force (Personal Magnetism). The person who has best devel-

It is this unseen element that often causes men, women, and young girls to do things in business, social, and even domestic life, that their ordinary common sense and reason would reject, realizing instantly that to do so would be improper, erroneous or injurious; yet under the hypnotic influence or magnetic personality of another they do it without thought, impressed that their acts are the outcome of their own natural, individual inclinations or desires.

Thus the world is ruled and man influenced by this psychic force known to-day as Personal Magnetism,

Force, etc., etc. Those persons who have this sublimated life essence well developed and under strict discipline of the will, are enabled to control, rule and sway their fellow men by the concentration of this force, transmitting or projecting it into the mentality of another, impregnated with the nature of their own desires, conserved life force, controlling mental energy or hypnotic influence. This fine, invisible, subtle influence or soul essence, which throughout India is known as Astral fluid, permeates infinite space, and is wholly imperceptible to the physical or natural sight, except by its effects, and thus apparently impossible to the materialist scientist, because not attributable to things material. To the learned in Psychology or Adept in OCCULTISM it appears nothing more than the ordinary and unerring law of nature. Thus this wonderful science of the human mind or soul of man, notwithstanding the nonsensical,

materialistic theories, and the foolish ridicule of selfconceited egotists, the tyranny of Priestcraft, and the ignorance of Godly egotists, is really founded upon that same immutable law to which the universe at large is subject. As to the absurd and foolish idea that is prevalent that it is superstitious, unlawful, or sinful, to study mental science, or soul possibilities, a moment of unprejudiced reflection will convince to the contrary.

Psychology is deduced in a demonstrable way from a certain chain of causes, which for ages past have been found uniformly to produce a corresponding train of effects. 

Where, then, consists its sinfulness, or its superstition, since the whole system of mental and Occult science is founded on actual Psychological experience and observation?

The earliest Greek Philosophers were in the habit of using methods having a strong resemblance to the Psychologists of to-day. The Ebers Papyrus gives authentic accounts of the medical methods practiced in Egypt prior to 1552 B. C. and certainly mentions the laying of hands on the heads of the patients as part of the treatment. 

Even the "Clairvoyant" theories of the mesmerists. have a history, as the early soothsayers and oracles depended upon the hypnotic states, and the acuteness and subtlety which the mental faculties gain in the deeper degrees of hypnosis, to enable the subjects to speak with a foresight and wisdom exciting the admiration and reverence of those who made use of their services. 

The Sibyls spoke many and great things with justice

INTRODUCTION 

17 and truth, and when the instinct which animated them ceased to exist, they lost the recollection of all they declared. 

Asclepiades was in the habit of putting frenzied persons to sleep by rubbing, and when these manipulations were prolonged, the patient was plunged into a deep lethargy.

One of the earliest and at the same time most striking references to the use of personal Magnetism or Mesmeric influence as a form of nerve stimulation, quieting influence and curative agent occurred in Solon and is given below:

Stanley (History of Philosophy, 1666) gives the fol lowing translation: "The smallest hurts sometimes increase and rage More than all art physic can assuage; 

Sometimes the fury of the worst disease. 

The hand, by gentle stroking, will appease. 99 Martial, touching apparently on some luxurious refinement, has a curious reference to the subject. Pliny refers to the method of fascination. Tacitus and Seutains testify to the cures performed by the Emperor 

Vespasian. Finally, St. Augustine tells of a Priest whom he knew, who could reduce himself to a state not to be distinguished from death. Most everyone has heard of the cures due to the "royal touch. Numerous cures were effected in this manner by the early Kings of 

France, and the "touch" was still in vogue in Queen 

Anne's time. Animal Magnetism and Hypnotic suggestion, according to "History of Scandinavian Kings,' 

---

was exercised by the Scandinavian princes, and particu. larly by St. Olaf, who reigned from 1020 to 1035.

There is no doubt but that the Egyptians, Persians, Greeks and Romans knew of the scientific import of these Phenomena, there being evidence of a common principle, nor can it be denied that many of the Phenomena were connected with the oracles and with the most learned Physicians. The first trace of any system which resembles that of modern magnetizers appeared toward the end of the middle ages; and this system grew out of the doctrines of astrology.

Some of the famous men of the time were at work on the subject, as the doctrine is advocated in all their writings, and the student who ventures to investigate will find them curiously fascinating and interesting in the extreme, though lengthy and ponderous. 

Prominent among these writers are Phillippus Aureolus, Theophrastus Paracelsus, Bombat De Hoenheyme, (the prince of physicians and philosophers by fire, Grand Paradoxical Physician, the trismegistus of 

Switzerland, first of chemical philosophy; adept in Alchemy, Cabala, and magic, one of nature's faithful; master of the elixir of life and the Philosophers' stone; and the great monarch of chemical secrets). Athanasius Kircher, S. J., was one of the greatest scholars of the middle ages. The works of Father Athanasius 

Kircher cover an enormous ground. There has been founded in Rome a museum named after him. For complete list of his work see "Bibliotheque de la Compagne de Jesus" (Brussels, 1890). Van Helmont, Sir 

Kenelm Digby, "of the cure of wounds by the power of sympathy." (London, 1660.)

INTRODUCTION 

19 All these men in various ways, at different times, have demonstrated the existence of an universal, magnetic force, by which the reciprocal action of bodies in general upon each other, and particularly the phenomena of the human mind or body, were to be explained. 

There were many systematic upholders of the view that the human will is capable of producing an effect upon the minds and organisms of other persons. Pomponatius, professor of philosophy at Padua, was born at Mantua in 1462, and died in 1525. He proved that sick men and disease were curable by means of the "Magnetism" existing in each healthy person. When those who are endowed with this faculty operate by employing the force of the imagination produce the intended effects by means of an evaporation thrown outwards. 

Health, according to the same writer, may be communicated to a sick person by the same means. 

John Baptist Van Helmont was born at Brussels in the year 1577. He was educated for the medical profession, but spent his life in chemical research. 

The discovery of laudanum, of the spirit of hartshorn, and of the volatile salts, are due to him; he also discovered the existence of the Aeriform fluids, to which he gave the name of Gas, a name they still retain. He died 

1644. His book was intended partly as an answer to

Glocenius, an earlier writer, who advocated the doctrine of magnetism, (but not in a manner agreeable to Helmont), and partly as a reply to Father Robert, a Jesuit, who had contended that the cures performed by means of this magnetism were due to diabolical agency. However, he made short work of his opponents. "Magnetism is an universal agent; there is nothing

new in it but the name; and it is a paradox only to those who are disposed to ridicule everything, and who ascribe to the influence of Satan, all the phenomena which they cannot explain." He defines Magnetism as "that OCCULT power which bodies exert over each other at a distance, whether by attraction or repulsion.

Wirdig sums up his own position in few but incisive words, taking up a most decided position; whilst 

Maxwells' "spiritus vitalis" indicates a great development, and, in fact, was a legitimate precursor of Mesmer's doctrine of the "universal fluid."

A study of the reports of the Psychical Research 

Society, the works of Hack Tuck, and Carpenter, together with those of Kircher, Van Helmont, and others, affords a demonstration of the relation of modern Hypnotism and Magnetism to their embryonic form. 

In the middle of the 18th century another comes on the scene, Frederich Anton Mesmer, attaining his degree at Vienna where he studied principally under 

Professor Van Swieten and Professor Haen, chose for his thesis on taking his degree, "The influence of the planets on the human body." Whilst at Vienna, Mesmer seems to have been attracted still more to the whole question of Magnetism by witnessing the wonderful "cures" performed by Father Hehl, a Jesuit professor of Astronomy at Vienna. 

The cures were supposed to be due to the subtle influence or fluid of magnetism which was imparted from steel plates and magnets specially prepared for the purpose. Having investigated the matter, and having satisfied himself as to the genuineness of the cures, he entered upon a series of independent experiments.

INTRODUCTION 

21 On one occasion Mesmer had bled a patient, and was getting the "magnet tractors" to heal the wound, when he accidently passed his hand over the cicatrix and was astonished to find that the pass of the hand had done what had hitherto been effected only by means of the magnets.

This shed new light upon the phenomena, and in 1775 Mesmer issued a circular letter, addressed, in the first place, to the leading academies. In this he maintained the existence of Animal Magnetism by means of which men could mentally influence each other, drawing a strong distinction between the magnetism which he termed animal and the magnetism of metals. 

The writer will not dwell long on Mesmer's doings, much having been published regarding him in this country. Sufficient to say that later he went to Paris where he constructed the famous "Baquet" or tub, which from all accounts must have been a wonderful apparatus.

About the year 1825 considerable was heard of Magnetism in Germany and scattered throughout there were many thoughtful and critical enquiries. One of the most curious points in the history of Magnetism is its influence upon the philosophy of the time. Schopenhauer, and other known writers, made Magnetism and its phenomena the basis of a large part of their philosophy. 

In most countries however, and in France particularly, the followers of this study increased year by year.

Again, there are several instances on record where the clergy practiced Animal Magnetism with no less an object than that of obtaining supernatural revelations.

Pere Lacordaire preached a sermon in 1864 at Notr Dame, eulogizing Magnetism as a power that would once and for all confound the infidels, and demonstrate to humanity the power of the infinite.

The church was constantly intervening in the attempt to check these learned men, and in 1856 an encyclical letter was sent to all the Bishops "contra abusus Magnetism," but this foolish attempt to check the then prevalent investigators and advocates was fruitless. England was rather late in adopting Magnetism. In 1837, however, the amiable but unscientific enthusiast, Du Potet, came to London, where he met Dr. John Elliotson of the 

University College Hospital. Du Potet succeeded in interesting his friend in his magnetic theories, and from this time Elliotson made use of Magnetism in the hospital and adopted it into his regular practice. 

However the council of University College Hospital soon passed a resolution against its use in the hospital and Dr. Elliotson at once resigned. The medical papers at that time are curious evidence of what bigotry and ignorance are capable. The most vile and indecent insinuations were leveled at Dr. Elliotson, who was at any rate, a physician of high attainments, and a perfect gentleman. Many of the charges and all of the prejudice, were due to the nonsensical ideas that were combined with mesmeric theories at that time. 

The writer will now pass from the history of magnetism to the beginning of Hypnotism.

Dr. James Braid of Manchester, instead of contenting himself with the Mesmeric theories, attempted to put the matter in a materialistic light. But of late these doctrines have fallen away and advanced students study

INTRODUCTION 

23 Psychic Phenomena from a purely psychological point of view, eliminating all such nonsense as hypnosis being due to physiological modifications of the nervous system (materialistic views). His desired attempt to place the subject upon a scientific basis, was a careful but superficial examination of the subject from a purely physical or material standpoint.

The Abbe Faria, who also was materialistic in his learning, anticipated Dr. Braid's efforts to a certain extent, but he seemed to lack method, and his language was vague and unscientific. Dr. Braid entered upon his investigations a complete sceptic. In 1841 he attended a demonstration given in Manchester by La Fountaine, a magnetist from Switzerland, with the avowed object of endeavoring to discover the means by which the tricks (as he regarded them) were performed. 

Soon, however, he was forced to admit that whatever was their explanation, the facts themselves were undeniable; but, unlike the case of Dr. Elliotson, the operator's true explanation that the phenomenon was produced and the psychological condition (Hypnosis) induced by the means of the transmission of a magnetic or OCCULT force from the mesmerist, did not coincide with his material views, so he, like many persons of today, set himself to a serious study of the subject and tried to account for everything psychic as the outcome of an abnormal physical condition.

The first phenomena that appears to have attracted his attention, as a possible clue to the secret was the fact that the subjects were unable to open their eyes. He attributed this to the exhaustion of the optic nerves, and was led thereby to the conclusion, which he failed to

verify by experiment, that the induction of Hypnosis was due to physiological modifications of the nervous system. Braid tired the nerves of his patients by fixing their gaze on some given object, a method which is very common to-day among a certain class of materialistic hypnotists.

Dr. Braid states that he found it was necessary for the subject to concentrate his thoughts as well as his vision, or in other words that "Expectant attention" was a necessary factor (psychological condition); but in the next statement he demonstrates his inconsistency and ignorance of the true cause by saying that the state is a super-normal physiological one, induced by a physical or appreciable action on the nervous system. He discovered also that verbal suggestion was sufficient to produce hallucinations, and this was a contribution to the subject, of the highest importance; but he had not fathomed the theory of suggestion, and knew nothing concerning its law. This was left for later writers. 

Nevertheless, notwithstanding his slight knowledge of the true cause, Dr. Braid was remarkably successful in the application of hypnotism to the alleviation and cure of disease, and it is difficult to explain how, after his death, other medical men did not practice suggestive therapeutics. Carpenter, the physiologist, supported him, and others of high repute. But it is probable that Dr. Elliotson's experience frightened him so, that he never attempted to force his theory on the materialistic presumably all-wise medical profession. 

Littre and Robin, and others, published extracts from Dr. Braid's writings in France, and Meunier wrote an article for the press. The interest was for some time

INTRODUCTION 

25 being purely theoretical, but nevertheless attracted considerable attention from men of science. 

However, in 1850, Azam, a surgeon of Bordeaux, tried experiments, suggested by Dr. Braid's writings, on a patient suffering from a spontaneous catalepsy. He also made experiments on another patient and found they confirmed Dr. Braid's conclusions.

He published the results in the Archives de Medicine. 

In France hypnotism was used as an anaesthetic; it not being known, however, that insensibility could be produced by verbal suggestion. Thus, only subjects in the deepest states of hypnosis were operated upon. 

From 1860 many competent investigators were interested in the science, and a number of works were published dealing with the subject. In 1866 appeared the famous work by Dr. Liebeault, entitled "Du Sommeil et des Etats Analogues."

At the time of its publication, however, it was received with much derision by the medical faculty, but within recent years a change has come over the scene, and Liebeault is quoted and referred to largely by every writer on hypnotism or kindred subjects. 

For a long time Liebeault labored at Nancy, but was little noticed by his profession. In 1882 M. Dummont, who had witnessed the methods and practices in vogue at Nancy, tried some experiments on his own account.

The results were so successful that he read a paper to the Medical Society thoroughly supporting Liebeault's methods and treatments. This combined, possibly with 

Dr. Charcot's experiments at Salpetriere, led Dr. Bernheim to investigate the subject. He, by his own account, set to work in a very sceptical spirit, but met with results

so striking and certain that he felt bound to speak. Dr. 

Bernheim already possessed a wide reputation, and his adoption of hypnotism as an auxiliary in his practice had at once the effect of securing for the subject a much more patient hearing than had ever before been the case. 

Many scientific men in France and Germany had interested themselves in hypnotism, but it was not until the school of Nancy dealt with the question that it took up a real position in the scientific world. Dr. Bernheim, who had devoted himself to its study with Liebeault, published his work "De la Suggestion," etc. In 1884 the school at Nancy became the headquarters of the leading French hypnotists. There Beaunis and Liegeois worked at the physiological and the medico-legal aspects of the question.

The contest between the schools of the Salpetriere and Nancy soon followed and every year has served to increase Liebeault's reputation amongst scientific men of all nations, whilst Charcot's school, despite the leader's eminence in his own branch, has become more and more discredited until, outside of Paris, it is hardly represented at all.

To enumerate the many students and writers in various countries would be a lengthy task. It must suffice to say that, beside France and Germany which were first in the field, Russia, Denmark, Norway, Sweden, Greece, Italy, Spain, Switzerland, Belgium, Austria, the United

States and South America, are all represented by many patient and learned investigators; whilst in England Dr. 

Milne, Bramwell, Dr. Kingsbury and Dr. Lloyd Tuckey have made valuable contributions to the study of the subject. The British medical association has found Hypno-

INTRODUCTION 

27 tism "worthy of investigation;" and seemed to have no difficulty in reconciling itself to the course recommended by the committee appointed to investigate and report on the question.

Mention will again be made of those who hold the idea of telepathy, magnetism, psychic force, spiritualism and other psychic phenomena. The Society for Psychical

Research is responsible for many in this country and England. The work done by Myers and Gurney under its auspices, must rank high, and they have provided much that claims the attention of the sincere student along strictly psychological lines. Edmund Gurney died some time ago, but the work has been carried on by Mr. 

Myers and others. This society has published some valuable records. Its work in this direction is certainly convincing, as the experiments are considered by many as conclusive.

In this country we have many learned and eminent men who are prominent in our leading universities and colleges, also men of repute in the medical profession, members of the clergy and private citizens of either sex who are convinced that the human mind has a separate psychic action, due to mental force; and that said force can be transmitted so as to effect the mentality of another whether he be in the same room or miles away. 

Therefore it may be fairly claimed that "Practical Psychology" (Hypnotism and Mesmerism) has now gained a position in the scientific world that makes it imperative to all students of Physiological Psychology, Materia Medica, and human nature to study the facts and properties of Hypnotism and Mesmerism from a strictly psychological standpoint. Upon August the twelfth,

1900, the "International Congress of Hypnotism" was held in Paris.

Many communications bearing on Hypnotism and Psycho Therapeutics were read and discussed, whose scientific character will greatly further the development of Hypnotism. In all there were five hundred delegates representing many different nationalities, which embraced college professors, medical and scientific men from America and Europe. It would certainly seem unfair after such authority as the foregoing that we should find so many wiseacres who object to a spread of the knowledge of Hypnotism. Yet we are all aware that there are some narrow-minded bigots, who make man a sinner for word-a race of unhappy fidgety mortals, "under mercury afflicted," who with loud boastings about morals and religion, go about to

29 condemn every look, word, action and opinion, that squares not with their narrow misconceived and selfish views of things. You must walk with them, or walk out of the world; for they are perfectly right. You must say their shibboleth; you must subscribe to their articles; you must observe their observances; you must kneel at their altars, bow to their gods, and listen to their materialistic counsels, or you cannot be happy here, nor prepare happiness hereafter.

They have chalked out a line of duty, so narrow that neither themselves nor other can walk in it consistently nor safely. They, therefore, pass through life, as if the ground on which they tread was composed of nothing but bogs and quagmires; and as if the azure aspect of the heavens was absolutely injurious to their sight.

Could one follow these people in to the hidden haunts of their own hearts, he would see what sinuous, serpentine ways, what obliquities of feelings, views, and principles, what secret longings, what mixtures and contradictions, make up their characters. Here you would detect the demon of pride lording over the soul, there the little imp of envy and malignity creeping around the heartstrings, there again the yellow-eyed fiend of jealousy infusing his poison into the liver, blood and heart's core of the wretched victim, while ill-nature and malignity riot in wantonness through the whole system. These are the kind of individuals that reject 

Hypnotism and Occult Science as an idle dream and an illusion, having never applied their minds to the study of it. They have entertained wrong impressions respecting its pretensions; and have imagined that the science professes to teach more than it really does.

These people should realize the bad aspect of Mercury with Uranus and the Moon. They have their Mars vitiated by aspects of Uranus which have declared in them a certain portion of querulousness, mixed with no small share of ignorance, arrogance and envy, eternally prompting them to find fault with what they do not understand. These persons, unwilling to be thought ignorant of anything, pretend to be familiarly conversant with everything; and they imagine they manifest their superlative shrewdness and incomparable dexterity by a sort of wholesale dealing in notes of interrogation. 

Thinking, as they do, that they individually are the emporium of knowledge, they grant nothing to their opponents; they concede no point; they admit no position, not even for the sake of argument; they are dexterous at a syllogism in their own favor; but uniformly reject the minor, the major and the ergo, from every other quarter.

They are generally smatterers in some art, or dabblers in some science, and having acquired a string of technicalities, never fail to pour out a most abundant portion of them into the ears of those who may not happen to make any particular science their study. 

But ask these persons a question, even in their own favorite study, and for the most part, the answer will be an expression of astonishment at your ignorance; but you must not look for a definite answer; most assuredly you will be disappointed if you calculate upon deriving any information of real utility from them. Are these individuals to decide upon a question of so momentous importance, so profoundly philosophical, so invaluable to teach to man, to the millions of society, nay, to the

31 whole world, as Practical Psychology or Occult Science?

A science based upon the fabric of creation; which has employed the master minds of a Plato, a Socrates, a 

Paracelsus, an Andrew Jackson, Davis, and thousands more whose names are immortalized by men of science and talent. That the spirit or soul of man has a magnetic virtue which may be accumulated, concentrated, and transported was the principal doctrine of Plato, Plutarch, Jamblichus, and indeed, of all the most renowned philosophers, sages and historians who flourished from the beginning of historical times to those of the early Christians. The Cabalists who were most learned in Divine things and derived so much from a certain text of Exodus, the Gnostics, the Therapeuts, the Mystic of the Medieval ages, the seers of the 18th and 19th centuries all held the same opinion concerning the 

Magnetic principles of the psychic forces.

The Hindoo Vedas, the oldest written scriptures in existence, repeatedly affirm the spiritual essence of the soul as having untrammeled freedom and action independent of the physical sense organs or the material body. This grand principle which is promulgated in ancient India and throughout the Orient was the foundation of the famous mysteries and magical ceremonies of the Egyptian Dynasty and other monumental nations. Possibly in the future when the materialistic shadows which so thoroughly obscure the vision and darken our institutions of science and religion have been cleared away, we shall have many qualified and impartial interpreters of unlooked for revelations concerning the past realities and future states of the human mind or soul. 

In closing this chapter, the writer would ask why the

occasional failures of some of the professors of Hypnotism afford any argument against the science. We are all aware, that to the juggling nonsense and knavery of these people, Hypnotism and Occultism owe much of the odium under which they labor. But men do not condemn the Holy Scriptures merely because heresies and heretical schisms have arisen therefrom; nor do they deny the heart of the physician, through his own inefficiency; nor explode the whole science of chemistry merely because the chemist fails in some of his analytical experiments. 

Why then should psychology alone, which, of all other sciences, claims the most serious and undivided attention, become the butt of incredulity, through the sins of some of its professors?

Let the student of psychology make his opponents answer this question before he proceeds to argue with them. Some say it is superstition. Ask the caviller "what is superstition?" And not one in a thousand can give you the import of the word "superstition.”

These are learned enemies, forsooth! They are butts for ridicule and laughter. True, the science of Hypnotism and Psychology has been used by designing persons, as a kind of fortune telling system; and people of no education have taken it up and some have pretended to understand it, have affected great wisdom and hence they have been regarded as a species of necromancers,objects of fear and wonder to the ignorant,—of disdain and contempt to the wise and discerning. 

Practical Psychology or Therapeutic Suggestion has nothing in common with magic or any like crafty art. 

It deals not in mystery; for mystery and truth are, for

INTRODUCTION 

33 the most part, at enmity with each other. The educated and true psychologists, hypnotist, and practitioner or Psycho Therapeutics scorn all idea of secrecy in the science. Those who entertain a contrary feeling, know nothing about its true principles. The hypnotist proceeds by a certain system, which all men may learn, if they will take the pains; though he makes no egotistical pretensions of infallibility, or perfection, in his hypnotic or "Svengali eye" and does not hold himself accountable for the ill use which evilly disposed persons may make of the power, his skill in Occult science, like real skill in every branch of useful knowledge, has a direct tendency to promote a spirit of moderation and reserve, of caution and prudence, incompatible with the arrogance and presumption of either a mere pretender or an opponent.

The writer looks forward to no distant day when Psychology and mental science shall have acquired such stability of character and utility, as to entitle it again, as in the days of genuine Philosophy, to universal reception, when its advantages shall be looked to as the main auxiliary of sound morals, rational philosophy, and true religion. In the meantime its indefatigable disciples, advocates, and admirers, will be content to have the sneers, the contempt, the ill nature, and the ignorance of its adversaries; and guarding against precipitation on one hand, and inattention and indifference on the other, will not fail to make those Psychological observations or psycho diagnoses of men and manners; of cause and effect; of signs and correspondences, as shall confirm them in the principles, and endear them to the practice of psychological investigation.

A practical study of the question shows at once how easily such fallacies arose, and became so prevalent, whilst the practical knowledge obtained by personal experiment at once disposes of all the mystical superstitions, placing the intelligent investigator on a sound and scientific basis. MAGNETISM, the life principle of Psychology, the sub lime force which constituted the wisdom of the ancients, the master spirit of antiquity, that imperishable psychic force or soul essence which can be concentrated, controlled, projected and imparted far away into space without the dreaded agency of death to release or liberate it from the physical body, is unfolding its true nature. And its magic revelations will enable mankind to achieve through natural law all of those seemingly phenomenal demonstrations which many have witnessed throughout Ancient India and termed miraculous. 

Unquestionably it is this same OCCULT, invisible, infinite element of supreme soul force, whose true principles prejudice, ignorance and superstition have endeavored to stifle and, if possible, to annihilate, that is the life element which constitutes man's inherent or lat-

37 ent possibilities. When this is thoroughly understood and scientifically applied, it will afford a key to the books of "The Holy Scripture," also to Hypnotism, Mesmerism, Spiritualism, Fascination, Magnetism, Psychic, and all other Psychological influences and Oriental Mysticism, which so well commends itself to the earnest student of Occult Philosophy.

Truly this universal spirit can by no means be so clearly demonstrated as by the sympathies in natural things, which are inherited throughout all nature; and, upon this principle of sympathy and antipathy, or the law of attraction and repulsion, is founded that magnetic spiritual power which tends to things. 

That there is a wonderful and magical virtue in the mind or mental forces, and that this invisible element can be projected as it were, from the body, when the powers of the soul are wrought by the focussing of the faculty of concentration, is clearly proven by experi ence. It is fully demonstrated in the action of the psychic or mental forces from whence there are most potent procreations, famous impressions and wonderful effects produced, which by many have been attributed to diverse superstitions, such as sorcery, enchantment, witchcraft, the Prince of Darkness, etc., etc.

There is inherent in the human soul a certain magnetic virtue given by the Omniscient Creator, which is natural and proper, as God created man in his own image,-"in the image of God created he him; male and female created he them." Genesis 1:26-27.

Now, inasmuch as we are this image and engravement, the soul or mind in this respect acts in a very peculiar manner, i. e., magnetically or spiritually on a person

or an object at a remote distance and that more effectively and powerfully than by any corporal assistance; for as the soul is the principal of the body, all action belonging to and coming from it is spiritual or magnetic, and of the greatest validity.

This high and sacred power of the Microcosmical spirit, which produces most potent procreations, is evidenced in pregnant women, who stamp upon their child the image and property of things intensely desired. 

This image or sign imprinted by the appetite or desire of a mother, on her young, does fitly, clearly and most certainly confirm a magnetic or attractive faculty, and its operation at a distance, for let a woman great with child, intensely desire cherries or strawberries, but, touch her face or other part of the body with her fingers, and without doubt the child will be stamped in the same part of its body, with the sign or image of the fruit desired, which ever afterwards does each year wax green, white, yellow, and at the proper time according to the tenor of the trees, look red. This wonderfully expresses and confirms not only an OCCULT action at a distance, but also demonstrates a conformity or agreement with the essence of the tree or plant,—a consanguinity or near affinity of a being or an object impressed into the body by an instantaneous action of the soul's natural faculty or Magnetism.

These signs or images which are conceived and brought forth upon the child by the power of the Microcosmical spirit, whereby the mother transfers all the ideas of her conception on her young, depend upon two consequences, i. e., that the spiritual principal, or essence (OCCULT virtue) of all things, which lie hidden and unknown to

39 many like fire in a flint, can be attracted at a distance and are born, as it were, and brought forth only by the working, power and phantasy of the magical microcosm. 

Again, the human mind or soul, in conceiving, generates from this protem or transferrable spiritual essence a certain sign, image or idea of the thing or being conceived, and in time this ideal spiritual entity manifests itself again in the material or physical world. 

We find another remarkable instance of the magnetic influence in changing the nature and complexion of living objects, in the history of Jacob. It is as follows:

Jacob agreed with Laban that he would still guard his sheep, provided, that Laban would give him as a reward for his services, all spotted lambs and goats that should in the future be added to his flock.

Laban consented to this proposal, and Jacob became immensely rich. It is worth the trouble to insert the passage relating to this transaction, as an application of the mysterious doctrine of magnetism. 

When Jacob would no longer watch over the sheep and desired to go away with his wives and children, Laban said unto him. Genesis 30:27-43: "I pray thee, if I have found favor in thine eyes, tarry. for I have learned by experience that the Lord hath blessed me for thy sake." And he said, "Appoint me thy wages, and I will give it." And he said unto him: "Thou knowest how I have served thee and how thy cattle was with me. For it was little which thou hadst before I came, and it is now increased unto a multitude: and the Lord hath blessed thee since my coming: and now, when shall I provide for mine own house also?"

And he said, "What shall I give thee?" And Jacob said, "Thou shalt not give me anything. If thou wilt do this thing for me, I will again feed and keep thy flock: I will pass through all thy flock to-day, removing from thence all the speckled and spotted cattle, and all the brown. cattle among the sheep, and the spotted and speckled among the goats; and of such shall be my hire." "So shall my righteous answer for me in time to come, when it shall come for my hire before thy face: every one that is not speckled and spotted among the goats, and brown among the sheep, shall be counted stolen with me." And Laban said, "Behold, I would it might be according to thy word." And he removed that day the he-goats that were ring-streaked and speckled, and all the she-goats that were speckled and spotted and every one that had some white in it, and all the brown among the sheep, and gave them into the hands of his sons. And he set three days' journey betwixt himself and Jacob; and Jacob fed the rest of 

Laban's flocks. "And Jacob took him rods of green poplar, and of the hazel and chestnut tree: and pilled white streaks in them, and made the white appear which was in the rods. "And he set the rods which he had pilled before the flock in the gutters in the watering troughs when the flocks came to drink, that they should conceive when they came to drink. "And the flocks conceived before the rods, and brought forth cattle ring-streaked, speckled, and spotted. And Jacob did separate the lambs, and set the faces of the flocks toward the ring-streaked, and all the brown in the flock of Laban; and he put his own'

MAGNETISM 

41 flocks by themselves, and put them not unto Laban's cattle. And it came to pass whensoever the stronger cattle did conceive, that Jacob laid the rods before the eyes of the cattle in the gutters, that they might conceive among the rods. "But when the cattle were feeble, he put them not in; so the feebler were Laban's, and the stronger Jacob's. "And the man increased exceedingly, and had much cattle, and maid-servants, and men-servants, and camels and asses. 

This proves clearly that the sheep and the goats could be made to bring forth their young changed in color and appearance corresponding with the pilled rods which were placed before them by Jacob as they drank from the waters.

In these days, the theory that the features of the offspring of a human mother can be affected by an object upon which the mother gazes, is pronounced absurd; and yet this theory, in the very nature of things, is as fully established as the fact that the mental qualities of many children differ totally from those of their parents. The fact that the sheep and the goats, upon seeing the objects which Jacob so skillfully placed before them, brought forth their young differing in appearance from themselves, has a very deep significance. Either Jacob knew what the result of this stratagem would be from experience, or it was revealed to him in a dream, for we read, Genesis 31:10: "And it came to pass at the time the cattle conceived that I lifted up mine eyes, and saw in a dream, and behold, the rams which leaped upon the cattle were ringstreaked, speckled, and grizzled." With the water

which they drank, and in which at the same time they saw their own reflection, they transmitted the image of the speckled rods to their young.

We have not the space here to enter into a more extended argument to prove the truth of this phenomenon, but the fact that the female progenitor, both human and animal, is capable at the period of gestation to transmit to her offspring the image and likeness of surrounding objects, has a surer foundation than is commonly believed to be possible.

The great army of materialists, who represent the spirit of the scriptures and of life as an ordinary earthly matter, so as to make it appear that nothing is hidden in the sanctuary that they cannot comprehend by their intellect, will never be converted. While those who rely upon the benign influences of a higher light in the temple, which will exist beyond the life of this world, will never need conversion.

Magnetism is founded upon those inexplicable or marvelous Occult sympathetic properties which are universally found to exist more or less throughout the natural creation.

Of the existence of these Occult forces or wonder-striking sympathies, which embrace the immutable law of attraction and repulsion, commonly known as objective and subjective, active and passive, positive and negative, there can be no doubt in the philosophic mind; as the vegetative soul of the world invisibly carries and inseparably unites a specific virtue from the starry heavens between one thing and another, ever working those secret effects which no reflective mind can fail warmly to admire.

MAGNETISM 43 In the present case who is to say or determine what the human soul or mind can or cannot accomplish when acting in uniform sympathy with the force, power, or element which produces results and acts regardless of man's knowledge or ignorance, of his compliance or of his disobedience to its law?

This is what makes an accident possible. If a person grasps a wire, through which a powerful electric current is passing, he will be struck dead. It makes no difference whether he is a materialistic unbeliever or knows and believes that there is such a thing as electricity or not; he has complied with the law of forming a circuit, the current passes through his body and death ensues. 

The student must have four principles thoroughly in his mind. 1st. That the power, force or element which causes or produces the results are the Occult forces of 

God's universe. 2d. That this power, element or spiritual force is secured and brought into action only by man's agreeing or coming into harmony with its law. 

3d. That the results produced are nothing more nor less than the manifestation of said agreement. 

4th. That all Occult force is like unto a two-edged sword, it cuts both ways. Therefore, man needs a knowledge of its nature and possibilities as much to protect. him from all of his own adverse concentration and morbid meditation, as he does from that of others.

Further, it is well for the enquirer into Occult Phenomena or the more abstruse forces of nature to know that it is the same Occult force or sympathetic power which causes the iron to approach the magnet, the waters to approach the luminaries, the planets to revolve around the central god of day, influences the

needle to point to the earth's magnetic pole, and by a certain OCCULT sympathy affects the unborn foetus in the womb, producing marks, signs or images corresponding to the mother's disposition, desire or fear. 

The same sympathetic instinct induces the new born child to approach the nipple, animals to feel the approach of danger, also of changes in the atmosphere. 

Thus by their actions we foretell rain or storm. 

The same magnetic or OCCULT influence which drives the frantic herd about the pastures, also affects the mind of the maniac and without doubt circulates throughout all nature, pervading all, disquieting all, even to the minute swarms that fall in the heavy dew, when the sky means to lower.

This universal sympathetic force is the same powerful influence that affects the heavenly bodies, and is the first pervading cause of every anxious and ardent doubt to which the mind is subject, or upon which the mind dwells with eagerness and desire, if possible, to know future results.

All instinct, intuition and mental impressions are the direct result of this magnetic or OCCULT sympathy. 

Again it is this same common affinity between individuals and various parts of matter throughout the universe which induces rats to forsake a falling house or desert a ship; ants to quit their nests carrying their young with them, before inundation; dogs to foretell disaster or death by strange ominous barking and howling.
